# 证据卡：伊隆·马斯克

本文件收录支撑核心原则与已知盲区的逐字原文。**引用块里只放原文，不放中文释义**
——中文说明一律走 `-` 字段，以免释义被误读成原文。

**每张卡一条引文**，附四件事：出处、语料位置、置信度、以及它支撑哪一条判断规则。

**置信度怎么看：**

- `high` —— 出自本人亲述的正式文本（访谈转录、演讲、著作、官方文件）。
- `medium` —— 出自第三方转述或他人整理（传记、书评、会议速记）。分量要看得轻一些。

凡引用，请照这个区分说明分量；标 `medium` 的不要当成他逐字写下的文字。

---

## 一、核心原则的逐字证据

### 原则 1 证据 1/2：第一性原理，而不是类比推理

> First principles is kind of a physics way of looking at the world, and what that really means is, you … boil things down to the most fundamental truths and say, “okay, what are we sure is true?” … and then reason up from there.

- 出处：What is First Principles Thinking? (Farnam Street)
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-fs blog first principles.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑原则 1「第一性原理，而不是类比推理」的判断规则：「遇到「这件事一直是这么做的」时，停一下。把这件事拆到只剩物理与数学允许的约束，然后问：如果从零开始设计，还会长成现在这样吗？如果不会，那么被保留的每一部分都需要单独说明它为什么必须存在。」

### 原则 1 证据 2/2：第一性原理，而不是类比推理

> We get through life by reasoning by analogy, which essentially means copying what other people do with slight variations.

- 出处：CNBC: Why Elon Musk wants employees to use first principles
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-cnbc first principles.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑原则 1「第一性原理，而不是类比推理」的判断规则：「遇到「这件事一直是这么做的」时，停一下。把这件事拆到只剩物理与数学允许的约束，然后问：如果从零开始设计，还会长成现在这样吗？如果不会，那么被保留的每一部分都需要单独说明它为什么必须存在。」

## 二、已知盲区所据的自述材料

本节证据全部取自他本人承认失误的段落，不是外部批评。**盲区用他自己的话来定**，
所以它比第三方的评价更硬——他知道自己错在哪，而且说得比谁都重。

### 盲区 BS01 证据 1/3：时间表系统性乐观——他本人承认这一点，但从未修正

> “I may have been optimistic with respect to the timing on some of these things, but I didn’t over-promise on the outcome,” Musk told me during an interview after the Model S launch.

- 出处：自我承认失误（BS01）
- 语料位置：`corpus/2015-vance elon musk 2015.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑盲区 BS01 证据 1/3：时间表系统性乐观——他本人承认这一点，但从未修正。缓解建议：他把时间表当**拉伸工具**而不是承诺用：目标定得远超可能，逼出速度，代价是预测能力归零。所以**用这套框架时，凡是涉及时间的话一律降权为「方向」而不是「日期」**——他说某件事某年能成，真正可用的信息是「他认为这件事该被优先做」，不是那个年份。要做计划，另找一个不以此著称的人做时间估算。

### 盲区 BS01 证据 2/3：时间表系统性乐观——他本人承认这一点，但从未修正

> Bowing to his nature, Musk set an insanely ambitious timeline for all of this.

- 出处：自我承认失误（BS01）
- 语料位置：`corpus/2015-vance elon musk 2015.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑盲区 BS01 证据 2/3：时间表系统性乐观——他本人承认这一点，但从未修正。缓解建议：他把时间表当**拉伸工具**而不是承诺用：目标定得远超可能，逼出速度，代价是预测能力归零。所以**用这套框架时，凡是涉及时间的话一律降权为「方向」而不是「日期」**——他说某件事某年能成，真正可用的信息是「他认为这件事该被优先做」，不是那个年份。要做计划，另找一个不以此著称的人做时间估算。

### 盲区 BS01 证据 3/3：时间表系统性乐观——他本人承认这一点，但从未修正

> Quite often, the machinists told Hollman that SpaceX’s timelines were nuts.

- 出处：自我承认失误（BS01）
- 语料位置：`corpus/2015-vance elon musk 2015.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑盲区 BS01 证据 3/3：时间表系统性乐观——他本人承认这一点，但从未修正。缓解建议：他把时间表当**拉伸工具**而不是承诺用：目标定得远超可能，逼出速度，代价是预测能力归零。所以**用这套框架时，凡是涉及时间的话一律降权为「方向」而不是「日期」**——他说某件事某年能成，真正可用的信息是「他认为这件事该被优先做」，不是那个年份。要做计划，另找一个不以此著称的人做时间估算。

### 盲区 BS02 证据 1/2：第一性原理在制度与人的问题上会失灵

> But -- but with the benefit of hindsight, I was wrong about the desire of -- or the interest in the existing shareholders to go private.

- 出处：自我承认失误（BS02）
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-sec musk testimony.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑盲区 BS02 证据 1/2：第一性原理在制度与人的问题上会失灵。缓解建议：他的方法在**受物理约束的系统**里最强（火箭、电池、产线）：那里真有客观极限，拆到底会得到确定的答案。但面对**人和制度**（股东、监管、舆论、政治），没有物理极限可供推导，「拆到基本原理」会退化成用自己的偏好重新定义常识——他在 SEC 笔录里承认对股东意愿判断错误，正是这类失败。**用这套框架处理涉及人的问题时，必须补一个外部视角：找来利益与你不同的人，让他先陈述。**

### 盲区 BS02 证据 2/2：第一性原理在制度与人的问题上会失灵

> But I was wrong about the level of enthusiasm that existing shareholders would have for going private.

- 出处：自我承认失误（BS02）
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-sec musk testimony.txt`
- confidence：high
- 现代转译：支撑盲区 BS02 证据 2/2：第一性原理在制度与人的问题上会失灵。缓解建议：他的方法在**受物理约束的系统**里最强（火箭、电池、产线）：那里真有客观极限，拆到底会得到确定的答案。但面对**人和制度**（股东、监管、舆论、政治），没有物理极限可供推导，「拆到基本原理」会退化成用自己的偏好重新定义常识——他在 SEC 笔录里承认对股东意愿判断错误，正是这类失败。**用这套框架处理涉及人的问题时，必须补一个外部视角：找来利益与你不同的人，让他先陈述。**

---

### 盲区 BS03 证据 1/2：对人的代价被系统性低估，而且这一点很少进入他公开的复盘

- 现代转译：支撑盲区 BS03 证据 1/2：对人的代价被系统性低估，而且这一点很少进入他公开的复盘。缓解建议：他把强度当作**可调节的杠杆**，而它实际消耗的是不可再生资源——最懂这件事的人。Vance 记录的大量受访者要求匿名、担心被起诉或影响生意，说明这个体系里**反对意见的传递成本极高**：这恰恰与他自己主张的「要能听到坏消息」相反。**借这套方法管团队时，必须单独设一条不经过他的反馈通道**，否则你学到的是他一贯的盲点，而不是他的强项。

## 四、算法五步的逐字全文证据

第二章里「原则 3」现在有整节的实战展开（见 `references/playbooks.md` 节 23）。这里放它全部的逐字依据——**这一章是语料里五步法最完整的一次自述**，包含每一步专属的错法，以及他本人五步全部倒序做了一遍的完整案例。

### 算法 P03-补 1/11：整体与次序

> I have everyone at my companies rigorously implement a five-step process for engineering. I call it The Algorithm. I'll list the steps, then explain. The order is very important.

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk（2026 年抓取，内容对应历年访谈与演讲）
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-the algorithm.txt`
- confidence：high

### 算法 P03-补 2/11：五步全部走反的代价

> I've personally made the mistake of going backward—on all five steps—multiple times. Many things at Tesla were automated, accelerated, simplified, and then deleted.

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-the algorithm.txt`
- confidence：high

### 算法 P03-补 3/11：第一步——要求必须来自一个人

> Whatever requirements or constraints you do have must come from a person, not a department. You can't actually ask a department. You have to be able to ask a person, and the person putting forth the requirement must take responsibility for that requirement. Otherwise, you could have a requirement made up by an intern two years ago off the cuff, or someone who isn't even at the company anymore. You must know the name of the real person who made every requirement.

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-the algorithm.txt`
- confidence：high

### 算法 P03-补 4/11：第一步能追出什么

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk

- confidence：high

### 算法 P03-补 5/11：第二步——判据是反的

> If you're not adding deleted things back in 10 percent of the time, you're clearly not deleting enough. Somewhat illogically, people often feel they've succeeded if they are not forced to put anything back in. But actually they have failed in a different way, because they've been overly conservative and have left things in there that shouldn't be.

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-the algorithm.txt`

### 算法 P03-补 6/11：第二步——删除的递归系数

> There's a recursive factor to mass. If you add an extra ton of heat shielding, you also need more fuel to get it to orbit, and you need more fuel to deorbit, and you need more fuel to land it. Also the structure now has more load on it, because it's carrying an extra ton of heat shield. This applies to any given ton.
>
> Adding each ton is almost like adding two tons when it's fully considered. We've calculated it to be around a 1.8 recursion factor, but we're probably forgetting something that gets us closer to two. Every one ton of mass begets an extra ton. That is why it is very important to delete.

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-the algorithm.txt`
- confidence：high

### 算法 P03-补 11/11：五步全部倒序的完整案例（电池包玻璃纤维垫）

> One example was fiberglass mats on top of the battery pack. At one point I was basically living on the battery pack production line, because it was choking the entire car's production. The first mistake was trying to fix the automation to make the robot better, to make it move faster on a shorter path. We increased the rate by 20 percent, then 100 percent. Then, we tried to optimize the use of glue and the drying speed.
>
> Automating was a mistake, then accelerating was a mistake, then optimizing was a mistake. Finally, I asked the battery safety team, "What the hell are these mats for? Fire protection?"
>
> They said, "No, they're for noise and vibration."
>
> I said, "But…you're the battery team."
>
> So I asked the noise vibration analysis team, "What's the mat for?"
>
> They said fire safety. It was like being in a Dilbert cartoon. I feel like I'm in a Dilbert cartoon quite frequently.
>
> So we ran a test: a car with and without the fiberglass mats and a microphone in both. No one could tell the difference. So, we deleted the part (the mats), which deleted a step that required two million dollars of robotics, because it was just a big pile of nonsense.

- 出处：The Algorithm — The Book of Elon Musk
- 语料位置：`corpus/未标年-the algorithm.txt`
- confidence：high

### 算法 P01-补 1/1：Colossus——把行业工期当类比拆掉（支撑原则 1）

> For xAI in 2024, we were trying to build a training supercluster (a giant network of computers to train an AI model). We went to the various suppliers and said we needed one hundred thousand H100s (superpowerful computer chips) to be able to train coherently. Their estimates for how long it would take to complete that were eighteen to twenty-four months. Well, we needed to get that done in six months or we wouldn't be competitive. So, we broke that down. We need a building; we need power; we need cooling. We didn't have enough time to build a building from scratch. So, we found a factory in Memphis that was no longer in use. The input power was 15 megawatts and we needed 150 megawatts. So, we rented generators and put them along one side of the building. Then, we needed cooling. So, we rented about a quarter of the mobile cooling capacity of the US and put the chillers on the other side of the building.
